## Attention Dismiss reworked into per-list Snooze (branch `kanban/rework-attention-dismiss-into-per-list-s-3cc2a3`) — 2026-11-10

- User-facing rename Dismiss to Snooze (buttons, accessibility labels, toasts, the card's marker, Unsnooze, tip text); the idea Dismiss and the question-form Dismiss keep their name. Internal names stay (`attentionDismissals`, `attention.dismiss`, `attention.restore`, `asks.dismiss`, ask state `dismissed`).
- Storage and behavior are per list: Needs you (`q:`, `b:`, `p:`, `e:`, review) and Updates (`u:`) are snoozed separately, a new key brings back only its own list. The card menu, sidebar and phone Actions offer Snooze in Needs you and Snooze in Updates, plus one Unsnooze. `attention.dismiss` takes a `list`. Existing records `{ taskId, signals, dismissedAt }` are read as snoozed in both lists and rewritten in the new shape on their next write.
- `docs/ATTENTION-DISMISS-SPEC.md` replaced by an English spec of the current design only; the rulings of 2026-11-08 and 2026-11-09 (blocker signal, per-ask counts, single dismiss, no Attention tab or Dismissed group, unread split out of "N need you") are folded into it and into DISPATCHER-SPEC §9. Also updated: SPEC-OUTLINE, SIDEBAR-BADGE-SPEC, ONBOARDING-SPEC tip row.
  - Checks: none run yet (docs-only change; `npm run typecheck`, `npm test` and the UI check are UNRUN).

## Slim board card menu, always on screen (branch `kanban/slim-the-board-card-menu-and-keep-it-on-d10cf3`) — 2026-11-10

- Cause (reproduced on the installed build, `docs/verification/board-menu-repro.md`): the card's ⋯ opened a separate action sheet with no Archive/Delete; the right-click popover had both but was cut off and could not scroll in a ~600-point window.
- One menu now (rulings, Noah 2026-11-10; UI-UX-SPEC §2, TASK-DELETE-SPEC §1): ⋯, right-click and long-press open the same `ContextMenuProvider` menu built by `client/card-menu.ts`: Open details, Start session, Move to ▸ (submenu with Back), Archive/Restore, Delete permanently… (danger, last). The phone sheet also keeps Move up/Move down (no card drag there; never on All). Switch, Link, Follow-up, Open workspace and Attention are not in it; Link and Follow-up were added to the sidebar ⋯ menu (`client/drawer.tsx`), the rest were already there or on the card.
- Placement: `client/menu-placement.ts` opens below, else above, else pinned and scrolling; the popover measures its height and the keyboard-opened ⋯ anchors to the button. Trail logic in `client/menu-trail.ts`. While a menu is open every board shortcut except Esc is ignored, and a drag is cancelled; the menu control context is stable (no board re-render per open/close).
- Strings: removed `Task actions` and `Move to: {name}` from all 9 catalogs; none added. Specs updated: UI-UX, TASK-DELETE, DRAWER, ALL-PROJECTS, CARD-LINKS. Onboarding/What's new/telemetry: no change (no tip or tour copy names the removed rows; patch-level UI tidy-up).
- Checks: `npm run typecheck` exit 0; `npm test` exit 0 (197 files passed, 1 skipped; 1990 passed, 7 skipped). Unrun: the rebuilt plugin in a browser or on the phone (the repro ran on the installed build before the change); Android system Back inside the phone Move to submenu closes the whole sheet (the host Modal has no hook).

## Review ledgers on cards (branch `review-ledgers-on-cards`) — 2026-11-10

- `server/review-ledgers.ts` (REVIEW-LEDGERS-SPEC): matching (`cardIdentity`, `LedgerFolder.find`), the newest-round summary parser (`parseLedgerSummary`), `applyLedger` (adds, updates the note, swaps or removes only `origin: "review-ledger"` records; actor `system` events) and `ReviewLedgers` (card open via `artifacts.status`, `flowly_get_task` via `AgentService.syncLedger`, a sweep 30 s after start then every 5 min; 30 s per-card throttle). Git facts come from the workspace descriptor, `git` (argument arrays) only fills gaps.
- `Artifact.origin` (optional) added; Flowly's records are `createdBy: "human"` so agents cannot remove or edit them. `flowly_get_task` artifacts carry `origin`; context lines say `[flowly]`. Card details: "Added by Flowly" and a sync icon.
- Host setting `review-ledgers` (*Attach review ledgers*, *Review ledger folder*) in Settings → AI features after Extra artifact folders; RPC `review-ledgers.check` tells the screen why the folder is not used. 6 new strings in all 9 catalogs.
- Onboarding: no tour change or tip (a background attachment with a default-on switch; it shows up in Outputs where people already look). What's new: none (no release in this change). Telemetry: no heartbeat field.
- Review fix round (review of 84c407a): branch and workspace-PR matching only for an owned, unshared worktree (`workspaceKind` worktree, `ownsWorkspace`), never the default branch (main, master, origin HEAD); a `prRef` card matches only its PR files; a branch ledger two live cards would match goes to neither (PR ledgers may go to several); card open and `flowly_get_task` wait at most 1 s (`syncCardSoon`); a read card that no longer matches loses its record at once, an unreadable one keeps it; a missing file is removed after two syncs in a row; people's and agents' artifacts are read inside the write; a person's role or title edit clears `origin`; "not fixed" and other negated words no longer count. The `<owner>-<repo>` folder layout's name collisions are noted in the spec, not changed. One case could not be tested: two live cards bound to the same workspace, which the board's invariant forbids. The shared-folder case is tested instead.

## Board groups v2, 6/7: flowly_manage_groups (confirmed) — 2026-11-10

- Task 6 of the board groups v2 plan (DISPATCHER-SPEC §0, §5.2; BOARD-GROUPS-SPEC §2). `flowly_manage_groups { action: create | rename | set_members | set_default | delete, groupId?, name?, projectIds?, revision? }` is in `dispatcherConfirmedToolNames` only: never preapproved, never in `savedTools`.
- Same checks and order as `flowly_create_project` (caller, switch, Paseo, `CONFIRMATION_UNAVAILABLE` in non-prompting modes); its own bucket of 10 per hour (`GROUP_LIMIT`), released on refusal. The mode check is shared (`assertPrompting`).
- `set_members` is one store write (`setGroupMembers`): listed projects move in, a left-out member goes to the default group, the default group only adds (`DEFAULT_REQUIRED`), an archived non-member is refused (`PROJECT_ARCHIVED`); the group revision is bumped when a member moves; moved projects get `projectMoved`.
- `delete` refuses the caller's own group (`GROUP_PROTECTED`) and runs `Dispatcher.deleteGroup` with `refuseLive`: a live session (running, idle, closed) is `GROUP_BUSY`, decided inside the start queue; a dead one is not archived and the group goes.
- Wiring: `server/group-writes.ts` (`groupWriter`) is the one set of group writes; index.server wires it into the tool. Charter: manage groups only when the user asks; each call is confirmed.
- Tests: `server/dispatcher-tools.test.ts` (confirmed, not preapproved, mode and switch order, bucket and refusal slot, create/rename/default rules, each refusal, `set_members` semantics and atomicity).

## Board groups v2, 5/7: one dispatcher per group — 2026-11-10

- Task 5 of the board groups v2 plan (DISPATCHER-SPEC, BOARD-GROUPS-SPEC §6, §7 step 4). DISPATCHER-SPEC's §0 list is folded into §1–§10 and deleted.
- Record v2 (`groups` map, global `previousAgentIds` capped at 100, `unassigned`, `legacyGroupId`) with the one-time v1 migration (session to the group with the most open cards, ties first group, independent of projects, run after the board's group migration, after a storage restore and lazily); v1 and v2 files are read, an unknown or invalid file is preserved.
- `Dispatcher` is the shared hub (record, workspace, one queue for start, replace and group delete); each group has a lazily created `GroupDispatcher` (held notes, cooldown, timers, start window, its own note batcher). `status`, `start`, `replace` and every `dispatcher.*` RPC take `groupId`. Title `Flowly dispatcher · <group>`, labels `flowly-dispatcher-group`, model chain group → host → default (`effectiveDispatcherModel`).
- Credential: optional `groupId` in the sealed identity, the group found from the record entry holding the token's `agentId`; `PROJECT_OUT_OF_GROUP` before the project checks on every call; `flowly_list_projects` and `flowly_create_project` follow the caller's group; the hourly buckets stay per session.
- Notes: the group is stamped on the fact when the stop is built and each group has its own batcher; `exclude` covers every id ever recorded; held notes (batcher buffer and held map) move on `projectMoved` and are dropped when the target has no dispatcher. Chat notes are stamped with `groupId` and `agentId` and a chat shows only its own session's notes (ruling, Noah 2026-11-10: this replaced the BOARD-GROUPS-SPEC wording "filtered by the project's current group", now corrected); cap and pruning per key; change line, cache and seen mark per group.
- `groups.delete` runs on the dispatcher queue: store pre-checks, refuse while running, archive, record, board; `DELETE_FAILED` on a failed archive; a late start writes nothing back. The interim v1 guard (`server/group-delete-guard.ts`) is gone.
- Client: the dock follows the shown project's group, else the default group (`groups` is now part of the board overview); the panel copy is group wording in all catalogs. Group tabs and the switcher are task 3.
- Onboarding, What's new and telemetry: none (one copy line; no switch or heartbeat field).

## Dispatcher messages a card's session (`flowly_message_card`) — 2026-11-10

- `flowly_message_card { projectId, taskId, text, sessionId? }` (DISPATCHER-SPEC §5.3): a steered, prefixed plain instruction to a card's session. Default session: the only live one, or the only running one; else `SESSION_AMBIGUOUS`. Refused with `SESSION_WAITING_ON_USER` on a pending permission or question in Paseo's snapshot or an open question or blocker report; it never answers anything. Gated like the dispatcher's writes; 30 per hour per dispatcher session (`MESSAGE_LIMIT`), slot returned on refusal or failed send.
- Rulings (Noah, 2026-11-10): preapproved like the other card tools (in `dispatcherToolNames`, so a running dispatcher shows the new-tools warning until Replace); cap 30 per hour.
- Prefix `dispatcherMessagePrefix` is an exception to "a card's ordinary follow-up sends exactly the user's text" (AGENTS.md), dispatcher messages only. Charter updated.
- Not verified against a real Paseo daemon: whether a steer could resolve a pending request (refused regardless); the live plugin was not reinstalled (asked-first rule). Per-group (5/7) is covered by keying everything on the caller's session; recheck after 5/7 lands.
- Tests: `server/dispatcher-tools.test.ts` (send and prefix, ambiguity, pending-ask refusals, gating, cap). Onboarding, What's new and telemetry: none (agent-facing tool, no UI or heartbeat field). Strings: none.

## Board groups v2, 1/7: groups in board data, invariants, RPCs, migration — 2026-11-10

- Task 1 of the board groups v2 plan (BOARD-GROUPS-SPEC §1, §2, §7 steps 1, 2, 5, 6). No UI change: the client still reads the v1 `board-view` fields; dropping them from the settings parser is task 3.
- Model: `board.groups` (`.default([])`, no version bump; a generic `groups` collection in SQLite), `project.groupId`, project `deliveryMode` override (`projects.update`, null clears), group input schemas. Pure rules in `shared/groups.ts`: name helpers, `migrateLegacyGroups` (§7 steps 1–2), `ensureGroups` (the §1 repair), `dispatcherTarget` (§7 step 4's choice), `assertGroupInvariants` (on commit only).
- Store: `migrateGroups` is queued by the plugin entry right after the store is built, before every other read or write; it migrates the host's v1 groups (raw parse in `server/legacy-board-groups.ts`, the settings file is never written), then repairs, in one write; a second start writes nothing. Until it has run, group writes refuse with `GROUPS_UNAVAILABLE` and no write adds groups; a failure writes nothing and is logged. After it, every commit ends with the repair and the invariants, so new, restored and unarchived projects are grouped; reads repair their copy only. A storage restore migrates again. Folder copies (`.flowly/project.json`) carry no `groupId`.
- RPCs: `groups.create/update/move/delete`, `projects.set-group`, `projects.create` `groupId` (an unknown group is refused before Paseo registers anything), `groups` in board patches. Refusals are coded (`CODE: message`). `groups.delete` refuses the group the v1 dispatcher would move to while a session is stored (ruling, Noah 2026-11-10; `server/group-delete-guard.ts`, replaced by task 5).
- Rulings (Noah, 2026-11-10), in the spec's §0: a stored "Projects" is reused as the default; a new `Projects` counts toward the 50-group cap; blank names drop the group; `isDefault: false` on a non-default group is a no-op. Decided here: an unreadable (not missing) settings file fails the migration.
- Review fix round (opus-reviewer of 4c169b6): a storage-busy migration failure retries with backoff during the run; restore and export move group revisions too; the repair also fixes repeated group ids and names (logged); a no-op `groups.update` bumps nothing; an unreadable dispatcher record refuses a group delete with `DISPATCHER_UNAVAILABLE` (real error logged). New tests: retry, non-retryable no retry, restore with groups and a stale group edit, broken stored list, no-op update, unreadable record, failed JSON save during the migration, repaired read and next write agreeing on the default id, `GROUP_BUSY` through the plugin entry.
- Tests: `shared/groups.test.ts` (every §8 migration fixture, repair, invariants, `dispatcherTarget`), `server/store-groups.test.ts` (JSON and SQLite: migration, ordering before queued writes, failed write at `save:written`, second start, every RPC refusal, guard, folder copy, restore), `server/legacy-board-groups.test.ts`, a plugin-entry check of the RPC wiring and coded refusals.
- Onboarding, What's new and telemetry: none (no UI, switch or heartbeat field). Strings: none.
## Archive PR review cards when their PR closes; `flowly_archive_card` (branch `auto-archive-review-cards`) — 2026-11-10

- `server/pr-review-archive.ts` (PR-REVIEW-CARDS-SPEC §10): `PrReviewArchiver`, a timer (first check after 60 s, then every 10 min, `unref`, stopped on unload) that archives PR review cards in In review with a `prRef` once their PR is merged or closed. Lookups: the GitHub connector (`ConnectorService.summaries`, github.com connections without `authFailed`) then `gh pr view` through `execFile`; at most 20 per check, least recently checked first; cards in workspaces with a running session are skipped; a check where no lookup works logs once. The archive is a store transaction mirroring `editTask`'s archive (actor `system`, note "Archived: PR merged/closed"), rechecked inside the write. The card's workspace is never archived: PR review cards never own theirs, and archiving it would go past the "archiving plugin records doesn't archive Paseo resources" invariant.
- New host setting *Archive review cards when the PR closes* (`archiveOnClose` in the `pr-review-cards` record, on by default), next to *Make cards for PR review agents*; 1 new string in all catalogs.
- `flowly_archive_card { projectId, taskId, archived = true, reason? }` (DISPATCHER-SPEC §5), dispatcher only, preapproved (in `dispatcherToolNames`, so a running dispatcher shows the new-tools notice until replaced). `flowly_list_cards` cards carry `prRef` when set (card sessions see it too). Charter: archive only when the user asks. The dispatcher test's name guard (`/replace|archive|dispatcher/`) was narrowed to `/replace|dispatcher|archive_(session|agent|workspace)/`: the ruling it guards is about archiving the dispatcher, not cards.
- Onboarding: no tour change or tip (a background tidy-up with a default-on switch; existing users see no new flow). Telemetry: no heartbeat field.

## Dispatcher creates projects, confirmed by the user (branch `feat/dispatcher-project-tools`) — 2026-11-10

- `flowly_create_project { path, title?, description? }` → `{ projectId, title, path }` (DISPATCHER-SPEC §5.2): a single-directory project from an existing folder through the `projects.create` RPC's own function (`createProject` in `index.server.ts`, handed to `DispatcherTools.createProjectWith`): the same board service call, dispatcher-folder refusal, native registration, duplicate refusal and guardrail command detection. Default title: the folder's name. Refusals of that path are `PROJECT_REFUSED` with the RPC's message; the caller check and `AGENT_CARDS_DISABLED` come first. Linked multi-repo creation and restoring a folder's saved `.flowly/` project are not offered.
- Ruling (Noah, 2026-11-10): the project tool is not preapproved. `dispatcherConfirmedToolNames` sits beside `dispatcherToolNames` (the preapproved list, which is also what `savedTools` records and `missesTools` checks), so the new tool prompts in the dispatcher chat and never raises the new-tools or unguarded warning; a dispatcher started before it lists it from its server and prompts too.
- Charter: create a project only when the user asks, from the folder they name; each call is confirmed; on `PROJECT_REFUSED` tell the user.
- Review fix round (Opus review of c0aed52; rulings, Noah 2026-11-10, DISPATCHER-SPEC §5.2): `flowly_create_project` refuses with `CONFIRMATION_UNAVAILABLE` while the dispatcher's `currentModeId` (Paseo's agent snapshot) is `bypassPermissions`, `auto` or `dontAsk`; at most 5 creations per dispatcher hour (`PROJECT_LIMIT`, its own bucket, refusals give the slot back); on the dispatcher's path a root inside, equal to or holding the plugin data folder or `<Paseo home>/flowly-dispatcher` is refused on canonical paths (the form's RPC unchanged). Relative paths are invalid input; `~` alone is home; the path is no longer trimmed; a blank folder name gets the title `Project`, and every refusal after the input check is `PROJECT_REFUSED`. The RPC and the tool share `projectCreator` (`server/project-create.ts`); tests now go through it (guardrail detection, one queue with the form) and cover the unwired, mode, cap and reserved-folder refusals. Unenforceable, documented: settings allow rules and a client's "always allow". Checks after the fix round: `npm run typecheck` exit 0; `npm test` exit 0 (193 files passed, 1 skipped; 1965 passed, 7 skipped).
- Second review fixes (re-review of 15a672d): the mode check is an allowlist, `default`, `acceptEdits` and `plan` (Paseo's Claude mode ids); any other mode, an unknown id and no mode reported are `CONFIRMATION_UNAVAILABLE` (`dontAsk`, not a Paseo id, dropped). `server/plugin-entry.test.ts` runs the real plugin entry against a fake host and checks that `flowly_create_project`'s `createProjectWith` and the `projects.create` handler reach the same board service and guardrail detection; with the wiring line removed it fails (checked once by hand). Checks: `npm run typecheck` exit 0; `npm test` exit 0 (194 files passed, 1 skipped; 1966 passed, 7 skipped).
- Board groups: no dispatcher tool (ruling C). Groups move into board data on a later card, which will give the dispatcher a group tool without a local-daemon exception.

A first full run had one failure in `server/auto-review.test.ts` (ENOTEMPTY removing its temp folder; that file passes alone and in the rerun; not touched here). Unrun: a live dispatcher showing the permission prompt for `flowly_create_project` and creating a project (no plugin reload in this task).

## Board groups: lanes are group-level (branch `group-needs-you`) — 2026-11-10

- Ruling (Noah, 2026-11-10), reversing "lanes stay per project": a group owns its lanes; BOARD-GROUPS-SPEC §0, §1, §2, §4, §5, §7 step 7 and §8 rewritten.
- Model: `Group.lanes` (optional only for data stored before it) and `groups.update { lanes }`. Each member's `project.lanes` is a store-kept mirror, so the ~25 readers of a project's lanes (Move to, start status, auto status, agent and dispatcher tools, workpad, auto review, insights, summaries, PR review cards, quick task, drawer, ideas) resolve through the group unchanged.
- `shared/group-lanes.ts`: `groupLanesFrom` (the migration union: statuses on in any live member or holding a live card of one, default order, a name kept only when every member using the lane agrees, all lanes for an empty group), `groupLaneCounts`, and `syncGroupLanes` (fill a missing group's lanes, turn on statuses holding live cards of live members and bump that group's revision, mirror to every member). The store runs it in the start migration (same write as the groups migration, so a second start writes nothing), at the end of every commit after the group repair, and on each read's copy. Project moves (set-group, set_members, group delete, the repair), project create, unarchive and folder restore are thus covered by one path.
- Refusals: `groups.update` turning off a lane with live cards of live members is `LANE_NOT_EMPTY` ("Move the cards out of {lane} (n) before turning it off."); `projects.update` with `lanes` is `LANES_IN_GROUP`. New groups get every lane.
- Client: the group board's columns and names are the group's, filtered or not (`boardLanes`); `dropAllowed` takes the group, so a member's card may drop on any column. The lane editor left the project fields (Edit project, the group page's project sections) and sits on the group settings page with Cancel/Save; its counts are over the group's live members, and unsaved lane edits join the discard question.
- Decided here (the ruling did not say): cards of archived projects neither count for the union nor block turning a lane off (a restore turns their lanes back on); the migration bumps no revision; the safety net turning on a lane for a live card applies to every write, not only project moves; the group's lane editor reseeds when the group's revision changes (unsaved lane edits are dropped then).
- Tests (written first, failed before the change): `shared/group-lanes.test.ts`, `server/store-group-lanes.test.ts` (JSON and SQLite: migration, idempotence, mirror after lane edit, `LANE_NOT_EMPTY`, `LANES_IN_GROUP`, editTask/placeTask/createTask refused on a group-off lane, set-group, set_members, delete, project create, invariant check after each), `client/all-projects.test.ts` (group columns and names filtered and not, drops). Changed: `server/project-order.test.ts`'s per-project lane test now asserts `LANES_IN_GROUP` (its turn-off and refusal assertions moved to the group test); `server/agent-service.test.ts` sets lanes through the group.
- Checks: `npm run typecheck` exit 0; `npm test` exit 0 (202 files passed, 1 skipped; 2206 passed, 7 skipped), on the final tree. Unrun: a live click-through of the group lane editor and of a migrated board (no plugin reload in this task).
- Review fix round (independent review of 6b6a83c): the migration keeps the members' lane order when every live member's full lane list has the same order (one member keeps its own), else the default order; reads fill in and mirror lanes without bumping any revision (`syncGroupLanes` `bumpRevision: false` in `repaired()`), so an edit at the stored revision still succeeds; the group lane editor is keyed on the group only and keeps its edits across revision bumps that leave the lanes unchanged, reloads when untouched, and refuses one Save after the lanes changed elsewhere ("Lanes changed elsewhere. Your edits were kept; review and save again.", new in all 9 catalogs; the second Save overwrites). Pure parts in `client/group-lanes-logic.ts`. Tests (failed first): order cases and the no-bump option in `shared/group-lanes.test.ts`, the read case in `server/store-group-lanes.test.ts` (JSON), `client/group-lanes-logic.test.ts`. Spec: §7 step 7 order rule, §1 reads, §5 editor conflict.
- Onboarding: no tour change or tip (the tour does not mention lanes). What's new: one 1.9.0 item (lanes moved to Group settings, an entry point existing users would not find). Strings: the lane editor's help sentence now says group (replaced in all 9 catalogs) and the What's new sentence is new in all 9. Telemetry: no field (off in this fork).

The step-through scope now follows the filter too (it used the whole group before).

## Board groups (branch `board-groups`) — 2026-11-10

- Named groups of projects, stored in host `board-view.boardGroups` (BOARD-GROUPS-SPEC). A group's board is the All projects board with a narrower scope: `pickView` takes group view ids (`*group:<id>`), `liveProjects` becomes the group's live members, and the step-through queues/counts use one `boardScope` predicate (all / group / project). Pure logic in `client/board-groups.ts`.

A deleted group's choice falls back like an unavailable All. Quick task on a group: last quick project when a member, else the first member; picker lists members first.

## Progress reads every chat of the card (branch `progress-by-workspace`) — 2026-11-09

- A card's chats (TASK-SUMMARY-SPEC §2.1, `server/card-chats.ts`): its managed bindings plus the live sessions in its workspace that Flowly did not start (e.g. a PR review card's `pr-review-watch` session). The workspace part is skipped when another live card shares the workspace; sessions bound to another card, archived ones and helpers (`flowly: summarizer` / legacy `ultra-kanban: summarizer`, `flowly-dispatcher`, Paseo `internal`) never count. Manual Refresh uses it; new `summaries.chats` gives the count, and the drawer shows "No chats on this card yet." instead of Refresh when it is 0. The server error now reads "This card has no chats to summarize yet."
- Auto summary: a completed turn of a non-managed session is read (labels are not on the hook event) only when a live card is on its workspace, then queued for that card under the same `summaryEnabled` switch. Session insights for that turn are unchanged. The Flowly MCP tools stay limited to managed sessions.
  - Checks: `npm run typecheck` exit 0; `npm test` exit 0 (176 files, 1756 passed, 2 skipped). The changed refresh-error assertion in `server/summaries.test.ts` failed first. Unrun: a live PR review card's Refresh and the no-chats line in a running client; the auto-summary hook path has no automated test (only its pure `cardOfChat` rule is unit-tested).

## Good idea → Quick task, and Discuss on ideas (branch `feat/idea-quick-task-dispatcher`) — 2026-11-09

- Good idea opens Quick task (QUICK-TASK-SPEC §10) when *Quick task for New task* is on, else the full form as before: a project idea, an idea whose card is finished, archived or gone (no menu any more; its reason line moved into Other options…), the menu's Start a new task and an idea link. The dialog is locked to the idea's project (`quickProjects`), its text is the idea's title and detail (`ideaQuickText`), and the card gets the idea's title (no AI title), the full form's `ideaDescription`, the idea's type, priority and tags, and `ideaId` (`quickCreateInput`; the daemon's existing `tasks.create` idea path accepts it). More options… hands all of it to the full form (`quickMoreSeed`). **Other options…** lists Open the full form and, for a card that can still continue, the menu's Continue / Send to another session… / Do it in a new session, in place in the dialog (the board's menus cannot show over a Modal). The menu's order is unchanged; its logic moved into `client/idea-choice.ts` (`goodIdeaPlan`, `ideaOtherOptions`, `newTaskFromIdea`).
- Discuss (DISPATCHER-SPEC §9.5): open ideas get **Discuss** before Dismiss in the drawer and on the Ideas page while the board can open the dispatcher chat (`discussAvailable`, the toolbar's rule). It opens the chat (desktop un-minimizes; a phone closes an open card over the chat page) and puts `discussIdeaText` in the message box through the panel's `fill` prop, an outside draft change (the native input remounts with it). It never sends and never replaces a draft: the text goes after what is typed, once (`discussDraft`, on `nextMoveDraft`).
  - Checks: the new cases in `client/idea-choice.test.ts` and `client/quick-task.test.ts` failed first (both files failed to import the missing exports), the three Discuss cases in `client/dispatcher-panel-logic.test.ts` and the new tip case in `client/onboarding-logic.test.ts` failed first; then `npm run typecheck` exit 0 and `npm test` (counts in the delivery report). Unrun: the dialog, the Discuss button and the chat fill in a live client (desktop or phone).
  - Onboarding: new one-time tip `idea-discuss` (after `first-idea`; pressing Discuss marks it seen) and a README sentence. No tour change (the tour's Good idea demo still turns the idea into a task). Telemetry: no heartbeat field (an entry point to existing actions). Strings: five new in all 9 catalogs; "Start a new task · Suggested" removed (unused).
- Good idea on a `scope=new` (or no scope) idea of a live card opens Quick task directly when *Quick task for New task* is on; the card's actions are in its Other options… (`goodIdeaPlan` takes `quick`; the board passes the setting to the drawer's `TaskIdeas` and the Ideas page). With it off, or for `scope=here`, the menu is unchanged (QUICK-TASK-SPEC §10, IDEAS-SPEC §6). IDEAS-INBOX-SPEC §2.3 needed no change. No new strings, tip or telemetry change.

## Dispatcher idea tools (branch `feat/idea-quick-task-dispatcher`) — 2026-11-09

- The dispatcher reads and keeps a project's ideas (DISPATCHER-SPEC §5.1, PROJECT-IDEAS-SPEC §4): `flowly_list_ideas` (a read; card and project ideas together; `status` open by default, `accepted`, `dismissed` or `all`; `tag`, `type`, `query` over title and detail, `limit` 1–200, default 50; newest first through the Ideas page's own order, now `ideaNewestFirst` in `shared/ideas.ts`); `flowly_update_idea` (a write; the card sessions' edit code, `changeIdea` split out of `manageIdea`; full `tags` or `addTags`/`removeTags`; card ideas' changes are events on their card, project ideas' none; `IDEAS_DISABLED` while Ideas are off); `flowly_create_card` takes `ideaId` and accepts the idea through the New card path, refusing another project's or a closed idea before anything is made. Both names are in `dispatcherToolNames`, so a dispatcher saved with the first eight (or `savedTools: true`) shows the new-tools warning; `firstSavedTools` is unchanged.

Ruling (Noah 2026-11-09): ideas of Done, Cancelled and archived cards can be edited and dismissed (DISPATCHER-SPEC §5.1). Tests added for a card idea's `origin.taskId` and for the dispatcher's own idea events staying out of its change line (both held already).

## Single pane final fix round (branch `feat/single-pane`) — 2026-11-09

- Dispatcher lifecycle (rulings 2026-11-08, "one long-lived session"): the `context_full` state, its panel text (removed from all 9 catalogs) and the start-fresh path are gone. `isDeadDispatcher` is the one test for a dead session (archived, closed or missing; `error` counts as alive, ruling 2026-11-09). A start is refused unless the stored session is dead; before replacing it Flowly reads it again and archives it only if it is still dead and not archived yet (that id only; a failed archive is logged and the start goes on); the old id joins `previousAgentIds` in `dispatcher.json`. Notes are never dropped while the dispatcher lives; notes in the start window are held until Paseo has created the session; after a turn end, held notes are re-checked at 2/5/15/30/60 s and then every 60 s until they go out, and a `dispatcher.status` read that sees idle delivers them. Note-drop counts are logged with a fixed label and the count as context. DISPATCHER-SPEC §1–§3, §7 (the remaining idle-read → send race stated), §9.3, §10; AGENTS.md dispatcher exception (dead-session archive).
  - Checks: the new `server/dispatcher.test.ts` cases failed first (9 failed: missing `isDeadDispatcher`/`RECHECK_MAX_MS`, no archive, no start-window hold, no backoff, notes dropped on a full context); then `npm run typecheck` exit 0, `npm test` exit 0 (172 files, 1609 passed, 1 skipped).
  - Onboarding and telemetry: no change (recovery behavior only; no new switch or core action, no heartbeat field).
- Dispatcher limits and gates (rulings 2026-11-08): the dispatcher's creations and starts count in their own bucket, 40 per hour (`agentCardLimits.dispatcherPerHour`), apart from the card agents' 20; the Agents create and edit cards switch gates every dispatcher write, `flowly_link_cards` and `flowly_propose_idea` included (ideas keep the Ideas switch too); a project with a missing root can be read, its writes get `ROOT_UNAVAILABLE`; `LEGACY_PROJECT` is gone (legacy projects follow the card agents' rules; charter updated); the change line leaves out the actions of every dispatcher session of this install (`previousAgentIds`). Interpretation: the 40/hour counts the same things as the card agents' limit (creations and starts), not edits, links or ideas. DISPATCHER-SPEC §4, §5, §9.2, §10; START-CARD-SPEC §3.
  - Checks: the new and changed cases in `server/dispatcher-tools.test.ts` and `server/dispatcher-items.test.ts` failed first (5 failed); then `npm run typecheck` exit 0 and `npm test` exit 0 (counts in the final-fix report).
  - Onboarding and telemetry: no change (no new switch; an existing switch's reach widened).
- Delivery mode editable until delivery (ruling 2026-11-08): Edit shows the Delivery choice, seeded from the card's effective mode and sent only when the user changed it; read-only (options disabled) while the card is In review, Committed or Integrated (`deliveryModeLocked`), and `taskEdit.deliveryMode` with a different value is refused by the daemon then. The stored `Task.deliveryMode` has no length cap any more. DELIVERY-MODE-SPEC §2, §5, §6; SPEC-OUTLINE line. Not done (optional): aligning `deliveryLine` wording with the skill text.

- `flowly_start_card` hardening: a refused move (the card changed during the create, or another card is bound to the worktree) archives the unused worktree as the meanwhile case does, unless a card refers to it or it has a session; after the create the worktree is read, and one that is archived or gone (the same key gave back a worktree a refusal archived) refuses with `START_FAILED` and asks for a new key. The worktree request (tracking branch, kept request, root Git check) is made after the hourly limit and the model check, so `CARD_LIMIT` and `MODEL_UNAVAILABLE` create nothing. An unreadable kept request is logged, left on disk and refuses with `START_FAILED`. Starts queue per project root instead of one global queue; root lookups run in call order so two starts of one card keep their order. START-CARD-SPEC §3, §4.1, §4.2 (the session re-check → board write window stated), §9; AGENTS.md start-card exception.

A first per-root queue that looked the root up with an unordered board read let two concurrent starts of one card swap order (flaky `two starts of one card at once`, 2 of 5 runs); serializing the lookups fixed it (8 of 8 runs passed).
  - Onboarding and telemetry: no change (failure handling only).

(2) Export: a note of only unbalanced parentheses (such as `(`) now counts as no note, so a title ending in an added-style parenthetical keeps it on re-import; a balanced `()` is kept as `added <date>, ()`. IDEAS-IMPORT-EXPORT-SPEC §7. (3) Start again's `NEEDS_WORKTREE` text names both causes ("…already had sessions, or Flowly didn't create it for the card…"), in all 9 catalogs; DISPATCHER-SPEC §9.4.

- Delivery mode locks for good (ruling 2026-11-09): the store sets `Task.deliveredAt` in the write that first puts a card in In review, Committed or Integrated (any writer: Edit, a drag, a status rule, an agent tool) and never clears it (a board restore from a backup taken before delivery rolls the mark back); `deliveryModeLocked(task)` is true with the mark or in one of those lanes (a card stored before the mark, no migration). The daemon refuses a different mode and Edit shows it read-only after a move back too. DELIVERY-MODE-SPEC §2, §6.

- Dispatcher folder out of the plugin data folder (security ruling 2026-11-09): the dispatcher works in `<Paseo home>/flowly-dispatcher/dispatcher` (`dispatcherFolder`, from the same Paseo home as the data folder), whose parent holds only it, so providers that auto-allow reads and edits inside the cwd no longer cover the board, sealing key, database or settings (not a sandbox: full-access modes and absolute paths still reach any user file; the boundary is the sealed credential and the permission prompts). Every start, before reuse or creation, refuses (`START_FAILED`, logged) a folder inside, holding, or next to the data folder: as given, on the real path of its existing ancestors before anything is made (a linked `flowly-dispatcher` creates nothing; a dangling link is refused), and on its real path once it exists (a folder replaced by a link is refused, reuse included); the folder and its parent are set to 0700 at each start. The stored workspace is reused only while its directory is the dispatcher folder, so one in the old `<dataDir>/dispatcher` makes the next start create a new workspace (refused while the old session is alive; a dead one archived as before); the old folder and workspace are never moved, deleted or archived. `ownsRoot` recognizes both folders (Add project, restore and resources hide or refuse both), and New task with an existing workspace also refuses one whose directory is either folder (the old workspace is no longer the stored one). DISPATCHER-SPEC §2, §10; AGENTS.md dispatcher exception; SPEC-OUTLINE line. Test red first (1 failed: `q:e-old` sent). Run natively with a fake context (no daemon): `["p:s1:r0"]`, exit 0. Not run live.
- Ruling on re-review finding 1 (Noah 2026-11-09): an unguarded dispatcher (no `guard`, or another version or provider) gets no board notes: `flush` keeps them held with no send and no re-check, and a start's board summary covers them. The unguarded notice now says it gets no notes until replaced and offers **Replace dispatcher** (confirm dialog): `dispatcher.replace { agentId }`, a plugin RPC only (the dispatcher's MCP server has no replace or archive tool, asserted). It refuses a guarded dispatcher (`DISPATCHER_GUARDED`) or another id (`DISPATCHER_CHANGED`), archives exactly that session (`REPLACE_FAILED` and no start when the archive fails), then starts a guarded one through Start's path. Start still refuses a live or closed one. AGENTS.md invariant amended. Tests red first (3 failed). - Ruling on re-review finding 3 (Noah 2026-11-09): the dispatcher is created with `extraArgs["strict-mcp-config"] = null` beside `tools` (the SDK makes a null value a bare `--strict-mcp-config`), so only the MCP servers Paseo passes load (its own and its plugins', through `--mcp-config`); Claude Code's user, project, local, plugin and claude.ai servers do not. `dispatcherGuardVersion` raised to 2, so a dispatcher started without it is unguarded and offers Replace. Probes (CLI and Paseo's SDK, killed at init, `docs/verification/dispatcher-tool-allowlist.md`): strict create and resume list the 8 Flowly tools and chat_polish's 2 and only the passed servers; the non-strict baseline also loads the datadog plugin server and 37 claude.ai servers. Strings: none. Onboarding, telemetry: none.
- Review (Medium): Replace archived the old session before the start's steps that can fail, so a failed start left no dispatcher. `run` now takes the session Replace archives and archives it after the folder, workspace, MCP server, board summary and record (a failed archive restores the record); only the create follows. A create that fails after it leaves the dispatcher ended with an error saying the old one was archived; the panel refreshes its status so Start shows with it, and the confirm dialog says so in one line. Tests red first (2 failed). Strings: 1 new, all catalogs.
- Review (Low): the panel showed a permission's text box when it had no options, but the server takes text only when it has no request, so typed text could be thrown away. Chat ask rows now carry `requestId`, and `answersWithText` shows the box for a permission only when it has none. Tests red first (2 failed). Strings: none.
- Review (Low): the text answer to a permission with no request now requires the session to be `closed`, as §9.4 says; a running or idle one is refused with "Open the card to answer this request." and nothing is sent or stored. Test red first (1 failed). Strings: none (daemon error).
- Review: spec and test for e8498c6's rule: a session restored from the archive is alive again, so its asks stored as `session_ended` come back open, counted and answerable (§3, §9.4). The code already did this; the test passed on first run (no red step). Strings: none.
- Review: missing tests added: an unguarded dispatcher's turn end sets no re-check timer (fake timers, running and idle); the first send after Replace leaves out the notes its board summary covers and keeps one that arrived while it was written; Replace and Start share one queue (Replace then Start, Start then Replace, and Start on an archived one then Replace: one dispatcher made each time). All passed first run; each fails against a deliberate break of the rule it covers (unguarded check limited to idle, covered notes kept, Replace outside the queue).
- Review (Low): check 7 now selects as the bell does: `attentionItemsWithFacts` over `board.overview-changed` with the overview cards' session facts (client/board.tsx), not `attentionItems` over `board.read`. For a check card, or any card, the two give the same signals: `attentionItems` is the same function with facts taken from the board's events, and the overview's facts come from those events by the same rules (open question and blocker reports, silent turn, latest turn); the overview keeps only the latest turn's `action` and `at`, the only fields the rule reads, and gives no facts for a session with no events on the card, which the rule reads like the empty facts `attentionItems` builds. Only the source differs (the overview RPC instead of the full board). Test red first (1 failed: the fake's `board.read` now throws). Run natively with a fake context: `["q:e1","p:s1:r0"]`, exit 0. Not run live.
- Ruling (Noah 2026-11-09): an unguarded dispatcher takes no messages from the user until Replace. New `dispatcher.send` RPC (the chat's message box, which sent through the client SDK before, so the daemon could not refuse it) and `dispatcher.what-next` share `reachable`: `DISPATCHER_DOWN`, else `DISPATCHER_UNGUARDED`, nothing sent, nothing marked seen. The panel disables the box and What should I do? and shows "Replace the dispatcher to keep chatting" beside Replace; a refused send shows that text (`dispatcherStartMessage`). The composer queue cannot hold a dispatcher message (its push and drain need a card bound to the session's workspace), so nothing changed there. Not gated: answering the dispatcher's own permission requests, and messages typed in Paseo's own session view. Test red first (1 failed). Strings: 1 new, all catalogs. Onboarding, telemetry: none.
- Review: Replace stored the new session before it archived the old one, so a crash between the two left a live unguarded dispatcher no record named. The archive now comes after the MCP server and summary and before the save; a failed archive changes nothing in the record and keeps the held notes; a failed save after it leaves the old session archived and named, so the dispatcher reads ended and Start works. Test red first (1 failed: the save ran before the archive, so Replace succeeded).
- Review: spec §3 corrected to the code: while the dispatcher is unguarded the whole chat is off, its own permission requests included (`requestsDisabled` follows `canWrite`, which includes the lock), until Replace. It also names the routes Flowly cannot block: Paseo's own session view, and card sessions' `send_agent_prompt`. Docs only.

## Live verification script for the single pane (branch `feat/single-pane`, Task 12) — 2026-11-08

- Added `docs/verification/single-pane-live.mjs` (checks 1–7 of the plan's Verification: dispatcher starts a card in a worktree, asks answered through `asks.answer`, one batched note, two-card split with wrap-up, backlog import, markdown export, bell/chat parity) and `docs/verification/single-pane.md` (how to run, what it creates, last result). Arguments and the check list are covered by `scripts/single-pane-live.test.ts` (the script itself is outside `npm test` and typecheck, like the other `docs/verification` scripts).
- Safety: every id recorded in `$TMPDIR/single-pane-live-<run>.json` before use; only recorded, prefixed cards inside Tinylink or the fixture project are deleted; Tinylink itself and unrecorded cards or sessions are never touched; messages go only to sessions the run started (the real dispatcher only with `--use-existing-dispatcher`); cleanup in `finally`, verified against the board and Paseo; `--cleanup <record>` finishes a crashed run.
- Written against Noah's rulings: no `context_full` check (a second `dispatcher.start` is refused while one runs; a new one replaces only a dead one). The one assertion that depends on the final fix round (the dead dispatcher is archived when a new one starts) is marked "needs the final fix round" in `--help` and `single-pane.md`. Delivery-mode editing has no check in 1–7.
- Checks: `node --check` exit 0; `--help` exit 0; `--only 9` exit 64; `scripts/single-pane-live.test.ts` 7 passed. Not run: any live check (Noah runs it after installing), and the independent milestone run after Task 12.
- Fix round 1: `--cleanup` now validates its record file before connecting (run id, marker, file name, fixture dirs strictly inside `tmpdir()` with the fixture prefix, card titles with the marker) and refuses the whole cleanup otherwise; sessions are archived only when verified by label; Tinylink's id and root are re-resolved, never read from the record; branches are deleted only as `kanban/single-pane-live-<run>-*` with no live worktree (after `git worktree prune`); SIGINT/SIGTERM run cleanup once and exit 130/143, and the retry command prints at the start; a dispatcher the user already has (any state) makes checks 1, 3, 4 skip without `--use-existing-dispatcher`; check 1 says when its archive assertion was not exercised; check 6 skips when memory is off. `scripts/single-pane-live.test.ts` covers each guard (23 tests). `single-pane.md` documents the `touch` pre-allow timeout in check 2, concurrent-activity noise in check 7 and the memory requirement.
- Fix round 2: cleanup decides cards from the live board (marker in the current title, in Tinylink or a verified fixture project), so a forged record cannot get a real card's session archived; a memory entry is deleted only in a verified fixture project with the marker in its title; `--cleanup` lists a recorded dispatcher as "left in place" unless `--yes-archive-dispatcher`; the cleanup report prints once, a signal exit repeats the retry command and says a long-RPC check may have created more; `decideDispatcher` follows the daemon's states (`context_full` is messaged, not replaced); check 6 skips when `memory.read` reports memory off or throws. 39 tests in `scripts/single-pane-live.test.ts`, including cleanup runs against a fake daemon.
- Check 7 fix (Noah's live run at 3122df6, 2026-11-09: "after dismissing in the bell" timed out at bell 3 = chat 3, expected 2): the check dismissed the card with `attention.dismiss { signals: [second ask] }`, which replaces the card's set (ATTENTION-DISMISS-SPEC §7a), so the first ask, dismissed in the chat a step earlier and still pending, left the set and reopened: one down, one up. The product matches the spec; the check now sends all the card's live signals, as the bell does (`item.signals`), waits for both asks to read dismissed and expects the count to drop by the card's open asks. `checkBell` is exported and run against a fake daemon that keeps the two dismiss rules (`scripts/single-pane-live.test.ts`; it failed first with the live run's timeout).
- Test hygiene (Noah's feedback, 2026-11-09): `docs/promo/setup.mjs --no-sessions` builds the repo, project and cards without the Haiku sessions; the script now runs only as `node docs/promo/setup.mjs` (importing it runs nothing; unknown options exit 64), tested in `scripts/promo-setup.test.ts`. The live script's help and `single-pane.md` say to set Tinylink up that way. Cleanup now also verifies the dispatcher chat and the bell: no ask or answer of any card the record names is left (`dispatcher.items-changed`; the bell counts exactly the chat's open asks). Not run live.
- Deleted cards' notes leave the dispatcher chat (ruling, Noah 2026-11-09), as their asks and answers do (`DispatcherAsks.chatItems`); cleanup now also requires no note of the run's cards. `server/dispatcher-asks.test.ts` red first.
- No strings, onboarding, What's new or telemetry change (a verification script only; no heartbeat field).

## Asks in the dispatcher chat, one source with the bell (branch `feat/single-pane`, Task 11) — 2026-11-08

- Spec: DISPATCHER-SPEC §8 (`asks.answer`, `asks.dismiss`, `tasks.start-again`), §9.1 ask/answer fields, new §9.4 Asks, §10 acceptance; ATTENTION-DISMISS-SPEC §7a (blocker signal `b:`, count by open asks, single-signal dismissal); SIDEBAR-BADGE-SPEC count and label.
- Shared: `openReport` (`shared/progress.ts`); `sessionSignalParts`, `addDismissedSignal`, ask kinds and states (`shared/attention-dismiss.ts`); `AttentionItem.blockers` and `parts`, `openAskCount`, `asksFrom` (`shared/attention.ts`); `blockerEventId` in session facts; ask/answer item fields and the three RPC schemas (`shared/dispatcher.ts`, `shared/rpc.ts`).
- Server: `server/dispatcher-asks.ts` (`DispatcherAsks`: live asks merged into `dispatcher.items-changed`, stored asks of deleted cards dropped; `answer` serialized, re-reads the agent and checks it is still in the card's workspace and the request or report is still open before answering, records `answered`/`session_ended`, answered elsewhere when the request is gone, never auto-allows; `dismiss`); `store.dismissAttentionSignal`; `readAttentionCount` returns `openAskCount`; Start again reuses `CardStarter` with a human caller (no agent-cards switch or start limit, `workspace_switched` recorded as the user's).
- Client: `client/dispatcher-asks.tsx` (memoized `AskRow`/`AnswerRow`: option chips, Other text, Allow/Deny or provider actions, report reply box, Dismiss, Open card, Start again; the answer shows with a spinner until the call resolves, the tick only on `delivered`); `client/dispatcher-panel.tsx` (`focus` scrolls to and outlines the ask; actions refresh the items and the badge); `client/board.tsx` (bell count = open asks, blocker detail, a bell row jumps to its ask when the chat is open); `client/attention-button.tsx`, `client/sidebar.tsx` labels say items.
- Strings: 11 new and 2 renamed (`Needs attention, {n} items`, `Open Needs attention, {text} items` replace the `cards` keys), all 9 catalogs. Onboarding: no new tip and no tour change; the `dispatcher` tip and the tour's Dispatcher line ("tells you what needs you") already point at the chat, and answering an ask happens where the user already looks. Telemetry: no heartbeat field. What's new: none (ships with the dispatcher).
- Checks: RED first for `server/dispatcher-asks.test.ts`, `server/attention-dismiss.test.ts`, `client/attention-dismiss.test.ts` (3 files, 4 failed: missing module, missing store method, blocker not a signal, no `openAskCount`); then green. `npm run typecheck` exit 0; `npm test` exit 0 (170 files, 1550 passed, 1 skipped); `npx vitest run shared/i18n.test.ts` exit 0 (44 passed). Not run: the ask rows against a live host (plugin not installed in this task): layout on desktop and phone, answering a real permission/AskUserQuestion, the bell jump scroll, Start again on a real ended session — Task 12's live checks.
- Known limits: existing users' bell and sidebar numbers change from cards to asks (What's new line waits for the release); a card dismissed before this change with an open blocker comes back once (accepted); a directory-workspace card that already had sessions gets NEEDS_WORKTREE from Start again.
- Review fix round 1:
  - Bell parity after an answer: `attention.count` is now `DispatcherAsks.count` (`asksFrom` plus the chat's stored items through `openAsks`): asks stored as answered, session ended or dismissed are not counted, exactly the chat's open rows. Client: `client/answered-asks.ts` keeps answered/ended/dismissed signals out of the bell until the board's live list drops them (marked when `asks.answer` or `asks.dismiss` resolves, and from the chat's stored items).
  - Ended sessions: an ask that left the live list because its session was archived, closed or gone is recorded `session_ended`, not answered in the session. Ruling applied: the user's Start again ignores `closed` sessions and starts a new one next to them, archiving nothing; running or idle still refuse (START-CARD-SPEC §3 step 6). Session-ended rows show Dismiss (stored as dismissed in the chat).
  - Decline on an AskUserQuestion records the reply's own behavior, so an empty submit is never shown as Denied.
  - Forms: one text box per question that takes text (`texts`); a single text for several text questions, text for a question that takes none, or text plus an option on a single-choice question is refused, never dropped. New string: "Choose an option or type an answer, not both." (all 9 catalogs).
  - The agent list behind the asks and the badge is shared for 2 s (`AttentionAgents`, single flight, failures not kept, reset after an answer or dismissal).
  - Spec: DISPATCHER-SPEC §9.4 (resolved asks, shared listing, known-path ended, per-question texts, decline choice, report resend after a restart as a known limit, dismissing an ended ask, Start again next to a closed session); START-CARD-SPEC §3.
  - Checks: the new tests in `server/dispatcher-asks.test.ts`, `server/start-card.test.ts` and `client/answered-asks.test.ts` failed first (the dispatcher-asks ones all on the missing `AttentionAgents`, which masked their own assertions; the start-card ruling test was also checked failing against the old line). Then `npm run typecheck` exit 0; `npm test` exit 0 (171 files, 1558 passed, 1 skipped); `npx vitest run shared/i18n.test.ts` exit 0 (44 passed). Not run: live host.
- Review fix round 2:
  - Bell and badge after a reload: new etag RPC `asks.resolved` (`DispatcherAsks.resolvedSignals`); the board polls it every 5 s while shown and counts `openAskCount(attention, bellResolved(daemon, client))`, so a closed session's answered or ended ask stays out of the bell with the chat closed and an empty client set. The panel refreshes it after an answer or dismissal.
  - `error` and `unread` signals are refused by `asks.answer` before any stored or remembered state, and never count as resolved, so a reused key counts again.
  - The board's forget effect depends on a derived `liveSignals` (null until the board and agents load).
  - Start again: `NEEDS_WORKTREE` for the user is worded for the user; `tasks.start-again` sends `CODE: message` (`codedError`, since only the message crosses the RPC) and `client/start-again.ts` maps `NEEDS_WORKTREE` and `CARD_HAS_SESSION` to two new strings (all 9 catalogs).
  - Shared agent list: the plugin host gives every RPC one `paseo` handle per daemon connection (re-created on reconnect), so it is not per client; the 2 s cache is keyed by handle anyway.

## Linear connector (branch `feat/linear-connector`) — 2026-11-03

- Sixth connector family `linear` (provider `linear`): Linear issues as read-only card inputs, with **From Linear** in New task, a Linear entry in the Artifacts +, and a Linear group in Settings → Connections. [CONNECTORS-SPEC §14](CONNECTORS-SPEC.md)
- Connection: a name and a personal API key, no address field. The key goes as `Authorization: <key>` to the fixed `https://api.linear.app/graphql`; the account check (`viewer`) reports the workspace, and the connection's address becomes `https://linear.app/<urlKey>`, used only to recognize and build links. New registry flag `noAddress`, and `verify` may return `baseUrl`; editing keeps the stored key.
- `server/connectors/linear.ts`: GraphQL through `ReadOnlyHttp.query` with `/graphql` as the only endpoint; every document must start with `query`. Recent = assigned to me and not completed/canceled, by `updatedAt`; search by key or link, by number across teams, or by title words (one `containsIgnoreCase` per word, all as variables). Read: description (Markdown as is), state, priority, assignee, creator, team, project, labels, parent, latest 20 comments. Batch summaries in one aliased request. Fingerprint: title, description, comment ids and update times; `updatedCoversContent: false`. Errors: AUTHENTICATION_ERROR/401 → AUTH, FORBIDDEN/not found → NOT_FOUND, RATELIMITED (429 or 400) → UNAVAILABLE with `Retry-After` or the reset header.
- Brand icon from the Simple Icons Linear path (#5E6AD2) as a 48 px PNG. 7 new strings and 2 changed (tour line, `jira-connect` tip) in all catalogs. README, skill text and ONBOARDING-SPEC tip table list Linear. Onboarding: no new tip id (the existing `jira-connect` and `connectors` tips cover it); no What's new entry. Telemetry: no heartbeat field.

## Switch a card between local and worktree (card "switch local and worktree") — 2026-10-29

- New card action *Switch to worktree…*: Flowly snapshots the main checkout (HEAD, `git diff --binary HEAD`, untracked files), creates a worktree at that commit, copies everything in, writes a handoff folder (`.flowly/handoff/<id>/`: `handoff.md` as a user input, old sessions' transcripts, `snapshot.patch`), moves the card (`previousWorkspaces`, `switch`), and archives the old workspace and sessions. Nothing starts. *Switch to local…* goes back; its dialog shows both branches with their last 5 commits and the changes per folder (first two directory levels) side by side; with uncommitted worktree changes the user picks *Copy* (applied as unstaged changes, checked per file in a few batch runs: already there is skipped, a conflict disables it), *Overwrite* (Copy plus the worktree's version of each conflicting file, the main checkout's saved to `main-before/`) or *Discard*; either way they are first saved as `worktree-uncommitted.patch` in the handoff folder and the plugin data folder. [WORKSPACE-SWITCH-SPEC](WORKSPACE-SWITCH-SPEC.md)
- Agent tool `flowly_handover` (CLI `handover`) stores the card's own files and notes; *Ask {session} for a handover* sends a fixed prompt to an idle session. After a switch the new session trims the worktree, then the user presses *Release from main…*: Flowly removes exactly the worktree's diff from the main checkout's working tree (and index where staged), after `git apply --check` both ways; backup patch, *Undo release*.
- Agent tool `flowly_integrate` (CLI `integrate`): fast-forward only, applied as a patch to the main checkout's index and working tree, compare-and-swap `update-ref`; refuses `RELEASE_PENDING`, `REBASE_NEEDED`, `CONFLICT`. The agent then sets Integrated. Skill text and `AGENTS.md` updated (new exception; the `git reset origin/main` recipe points switched cards to `flowly_integrate`).
- History follows the card: archived sessions, usage, insights and workflow insights also read the earlier workspaces; their archived sessions open read-only.
- 58 new strings in all catalogs. Onboarding: a *Switch to a worktree* line in the tour's More features; no tip (the release notice explains the only non-obvious step). Telemetry: none.
- Checks: `server/workspace-git.test.ts` (15), `server/workspace-switch.test.ts` (12), `shared/workspace-switch.test.ts` (3), handover write in `server/agent-service.test.ts`, CLI refusals in `server/tool-server.test.ts`; `npm run typecheck` and `npm test` on HEAD plus this card's changes in a temporary worktree. Live check (§15) not run yet.

## Auto review reads every repository of a linked root — 2026-11-07

- Bug: in a linked root (PROJECT-PICKER-SPEC) the workspace directory is not a Git repository, so the review's `git diff`, HEAD and `ls-files` were empty, the shape was 0 and the commands ran in a folder without code (Admin+SMITH, 2026-10-30: "diff is empty, this workspace is not a git repository").
- [GUARDRAILS-SPEC](GUARDRAILS-SPEC.md) §6.2, §6.3: when the directory is not a repository, `linkedRepos` reads its non-hidden children (link or folder) that hold `.git` (at most 20). Shape (`linkedShape`) is their sum; the review diff (`linkedReviewDiff`) has one section per repository with every path under its name (`--src-prefix`/`--dst-prefix`), each repository cut to an equal share of the 64 KiB (the live dry run on Admin-SMITH showed agent-smith's own uncommitted diff filling all 64 KiB and hiding search-admin), so findings, B1 evidence (the file exists through the link) and rework messages cite `SMITH/src/x.ts`. `fingerprint.repos` (new optional field in `shared/model.ts`) holds each repository's sha; `fingerprint.sha` is a digest of them; round 2 diffs each repository since its own sha.
- Commands (`commandApplies`, `runInRepos`): each runs in the repositories that have it (package script, makefile target, `go.mod`, `Cargo.toml`; anything else everywhere), in order, until one fails; none applies → `skipped`, never Unavailable. A directory that is a repository behaves as before.
- Commands run only in the repositories this delivery changed (`changedRepos`: uncommitted or untracked changes, or a commit after the card was created; a repository git cannot read counts; none changed → all). The idea's first rule, "its paths are in the shape", could not narrow anything: without a base the shape includes each repository's last five commits (D6), so every repository with history counts. The user chose the rule above (2026-11-07). In Admin+SMITH today both repositories have uncommitted work from elsewhere, so both still run.
- Where a command ran (2026-11-07, after PR #93): in a linked root each command item's `detail` is `Ran in <repo>, <repo>.` (the repositories it ran in, a failing one last), passed or not; the review drawer shows it under the command when the row is opened (`client/task-review.tsx`), and `flowly_get_task` already passes `detail` to the agent. A directory that is a repository writes none. Live on Admin+SMITH (`make test` added temporarily, Ask SMITH rerun, then removed and Auto review turned back off): `detail` was `Ran in agent-smith.`.
- Onboarding and telemetry: none (internal correctness fix, nothing new to learn or count).
- Checks: `npm run typecheck` exit 0; `npm test` exit 0; new tests on real temporary repositories behind symlinks. Live on Admin+SMITH (Paseo 0.15.3, Auto review and two commands added temporarily, then removed): shape 282 files instead of 0, `fingerprint.repos` for both repositories, a finding citing `agent-smith/docs/…`, `make test` passed in agent-smith and `npm run check-types` in search-admin (the root has neither a Makefile nor a package.json).

## PR #75 review fixes: quote evidence, one timeout across refused permissions — 2026-11-07

- Auto review B1 (CodeRabbit, Major): a failed check whose only evidence was a quote was accepted even when the quote was invented. A quote now counts only when it appears, after collapsing runs of whitespace and ignoring case, as a substring of the card title, the card description, a message of the filtered conversation the reviewer was given, or that check's own user words (`hasEvidence` in `shared/auto-review.ts`, sources built in `server/auto-review.ts` `judge`). Otherwise the verdict becomes `na` with reason `no evidence`. File evidence (a file in the diff or an existing repository file) is unchanged and still counts alongside an invented quote. GUARDRAILS-SPEC §0.1 B1 is to be updated by its owner. Tests in `shared/auto-review.test.ts` and `server/auto-review.test.ts`.
- `finishRefusingPermissions` (`server/summary-models.ts`): the timeout restarted after each refused permission, so a run could take up to 21 × the timeout. One deadline now covers the whole run; each wait gets the remaining time, and a run past the deadline ends as `timeout`. Test in `server/summary-models.test.ts`.
- Onboarding and telemetry: none (internal correctness fixes).

## Temporary sessions run Claude in Plan Mode — 2026-10-30

- Bug follow-up 2 (2026-10-30): the Ask SMITH card sat in Attention with nothing to do because its review ended `unavailable` ("asked for a permission") and that signal stays until the card moves. Plan Mode only helps Claude profiles; a Codex review session still stopped on its first tool call. `finishRefusingPermissions` (`server/summary-models.ts`, used by `runSummaryModels` and the workflow-insights run) now answers each pending permission request with deny and waits for the turn again, up to 20 times, so no temporary session fails on a permission anymore. Tests in `server/summary-models.test.ts` (refused and continues; unrefusable and endless still fail). Unavailable still raises Attention for real failures (spec §3.2); I left that as designed. AUTO-REVIEW-SPEC §4.4, TASK-SUMMARY-SPEC §2.
- Bug follow-up (2026-10-30): the same error came back as "claude-compass-smith-sqp/claude-sonnet-5-5: The summary model asked for a permission." `readOnlyMode` only matched the provider id `claude`, so Claude Code profiles named `claude-*` (SMITH/RCMD/KB SQP, AICodeWith) still ran in Always Ask. It now matches `claude` and `claude-*`; `codex-*` profiles stay on their default. Test added in `shared/summary.test.ts`. The match is by name, not by the provider's advertised modes (the SDK's provider list does not carry them without another call per provider).
- Bug: an auto review ended `unavailable` with "claude/claude-haiku-5-5: The summary model asked for a permission." The review session was created without a mode, so Claude ran in Always Ask and its first tool call blocked on a permission. `readOnlyMode` / `readOnlyConfig` in `shared/summary.ts` give `claude/*` the `plan` mode and leave other providers on their default (Codex has no read-only mode; never full access). Used by the review session, the card summarizer, the memory finished-card review, the Insights digest, workflow insights, the guardrails catch-up and the outside-session insights run (it did not carry the parent's mode). AUTO-REVIEW-SPEC §4.4, TASK-SUMMARY-SPEC §2.
- Onboarding: no tour update or tip is warranted; this is an internal reliability fix with nothing new for the user to learn.
- Unrun: a live Claude review after the change (needs a plugin reload).

## Guardrails rework after the live verification — 2026-10-29

Three findings of [the live verification](verification/guardrails-live-2026-10-29/REPORT.md) (items 2.3, 2.6 and the shape note):

- The change shape counted the wrong commits (GUARDRAILS-SPEC §6.2): `git diff HEAD` plus `git log -5` on a worktree card is the base branch's history, so every Tinylink card measured `m`. A worktree card now stores `task.baseRef` at creation (the ref `resolveWorktreeBaseRef` already resolved; `Task` had no such field, so cards created earlier fall back), and `reviewShape` / `reviewDiff` take its change from `git merge-base <baseRef> HEAD` (`git diff <base> --numstat`, round 1 diff `git diff <base>`). Directory, linked and older cards, or a failed merge-base, keep the old method; round ≥ 2 keeps `git diff <previous sha>`.
- Untracked files were invisible (§6.2, §6.3): a 450-line new fixture measured `m` and the reviewer never saw it. Shape and diff now add `git ls-files --others --exclude-standard -z` in every mode: one file each, text lines counted (a NUL in the first 8 KiB is binary, 0 lines), and the diff appends `git diff --no-index -- /dev/null <file>` under `# New files (untracked)` before the 64 KiB cut. The index is never touched.
- Corrections were judged too strictly (§4.2): "Adding a new requirement is not a correction" made the model classify `新接口也要写进 README` / `新文案也要进所有语言` as none. The rule now says a message that the delivered change should also / still / should have done something that could have been part of it is a correction even when short; only unrelated work (another feature, another page) is a new requirement.
- Follow-up (same day): untracked files over 1 MiB count as one file with 0 lines and are only named in the review diff; PR #75 review threads: a failed Auto review write after Create project toasts and still closes the form (no duplicate project), the start-up migration's rejection is logged, and a failed migration now refuses guardrails writes (reads stay empty) so the next start migrates again instead of finding a `guardrails.json` that the first write had created; hook writers log that refusal with `logFailure`.

## Guardrails phase B: the delivery review and the agent surface (card 2458cff4) — 2026-10-28

- Review (GUARDRAILS-SPEC §6): `AutoReviewService` reads the project's guardrails at start (`GuardrailsService.reviewInput`): active and observing commands always, the built-in goal always, and while Guardrails is on (B4) the active guidelines in scope for the card's type and this delivery's size plus the card's open items. The delivery's shape comes from `git diff HEAD --numstat` plus the last five commits (`reviewShape`, binary 0, renames to the new path) and is stored as `review.shape`; `review.items` replaces `rules` (old records read their `rules` as items; `rules` stays optional and is no longer written). Observing commands run with `gating: false` and never decide the verdict (`reviewVerdict`). One review session judges every queued check and returns `{items:[{id, verdict, detail, file?, line?, quote?}]}`; a `fail` without a file in the diff, an existing repository file or a quote becomes `na` with reason `no evidence` (B1, `hasEvidence`, paths kept inside the workspace). From round 2 the session reads `git diff <previous sha>` and the previous results; a check that passed before and gets no verdict keeps its pass. Model: the Review model, else the summary models, never the project's agent model. `review_finished.results` carry `kind` (and `gating: false` for observing commands, so Insights can tell the verdict).
- Disputes (§6.1, B7): `flowly_update_status(in_review)` takes `disputes: [{id, reason}]` (ids of the latest review's failed items or ones already disputed, idempotent, at most 10, else `INVALID_INPUT`; only with a delivery), stored in `task.disputes` without a task revision; the next review records them as `disputed` with the reason and does not judge them.
- Signals and retirement at review end (§6.6, §6.7, `GuardrailsService.reviewed`): reviews/passes per command and guideline, caught (failed in the card's previous review, passed now, not disputed), `unavailableRun` and observation counts for commands; then `retirement()` over the project's entries with `guardrail_retired` events for pauses and archives (an observing command that earned it becomes active silently). Counts do not change an entry's revision; state changes do. A cancelled review counts nothing.
- Rework message (§6.5): guidelines and items in the user's words, each failed line with `[id: …]` for disputes, the acceptance-list and `disputes=[{id, reason}]` sentence.
- Delivery gate and workpad (§6.1, §7.1): `WORKPAD_OPEN` and `WORKPAD_FAILING` are gone; `acknowledgeOpen` only answers `SETUP_INCOMPLETE`, whose `workpad` item now means "no acceptance list". `Workpad` holds `acceptance`, `revision` and who/when (old `validation`, `confusions`, `env`, `head`, `delivery` are dropped when read); `flowly_update_workpad` and CLI `workpad` take only `acceptance` (anything else is `WORKPAD_INVALID`); `workpads.check` only ticks acceptance; `workpads.resolve-confusion` and `workpads.refresh` removed with the HEAD refresh. Plan snapshots unchanged. Card signal is `Acceptance n/m`.
- Settings and removals (§7.5, §8): `project.autoReview` is `{enabled, maxRounds}` (stored `rules`/`checks`/`goal`/`guidance` kept on disk for the one-time migration and ignored; the next save drops them), `projects.update` accepts only those two; `project.reviewDraft` and `task.reviewSetup` dropped. Removed: `flowly_propose_review_rules` (MCP, CLI `review-rules`, receipts kept readable), `review_rules_proposed`, RPCs `reviews.detect-commands|analyze-history|analysis-status|apply-draft|discard-draft|setup-card`, `insights.add-review-check|pause-review-check|dismiss-review-nudge`, `server/review-history.ts`, `shared/review-history.ts`, `review_rule`/`review_pause` findings (stored ones dropped when read) and `reviewNudges`.
- Weekly run (§4.5, §7.3), inside the Insights run before the model call: with Auto review on, commands whose package script or Makefile target is gone pause (`script missing`) and whitelisted checks (`test*`, `lint*`, `typecheck`, `type-check`, `tsc`, `check`, `vet`) that agents ran on ≥ 3 cards in 30 days (command log keys, any package manager's spelling) join as observing; retirement over every entry; with card summaries off, one Summary-model call in the background workspace over up to 30 `feedback` clues of the last 7 days learns through `GuardrailsService.learn` (`learnedUntil` per project avoids learning twice). The model reads a `# Guardrails` section (guidelines with scope, counts, source cards and "caught 5+ in 30 days", notes, open items, the last 30 days' review results) and may return `merges` (≤ 5, both ids active in the project and of one kind; drop archived `merged` with `supersededBy`, counts summed, sources merged, scope union, `guardrail_retired` event). Anything else the model invents (a guideline, a command, a `review_rule`) is dropped.
- Agent surface (§5.2, §5.3, §9): `get_task` has no `reviewRules`; `review` lists items with kind, gating and dispute reason; `workpad` is `{revision, acceptance}`. `flowly_recall` also returns matching active guidelines (`guidelines`). Skill, turn checklist and tool texts per §9; CLI `status in_review --disputes '<JSON>'`.
- Telemetry (§10): `f_auto_review_llm` removed; `c_guardrails` (active guidelines across unarchived projects, bucketed like `projects`); TELEMETRY-SPEC §4 updated, `c_memory_confirmed` wording now says newly active notes.
- For phase C: `reviewProgress(review)` in `shared/auto-review.ts` (§7.1.1) and `insights.guardrails` (`guardrailsLedger` and `guardrailsLedgerSchema` in `shared/guardrails.ts`). Client edits made only to keep typecheck green are listed in the phase B report; `client/phase-b-orphans.ts` (never imported) keeps the catalog test green for the strings whose UI phase B removed, for phase C to delete with those catalog entries.
- Not done here: Insights "a new command found past the cap" line (§3.4, no channel yet), review `tokens` (the SDK result gives none), the misfire count for disputed goal/card items (no entry to count on).

## Guardrails phase A: foundation (card 2458cff4) — 2026-10-28

- [GUARDRAILS-SPEC](GUARDRAILS-SPEC.md) §3: `guardrails.json` (`shared/guardrails.ts` schemas, limits and pure logic; `server/guardrails.ts` store with serial writes, `.bak`, atomic replace, invalid file kept). One-time migration at daemon start from `memory.json` and every project's `autoReview.rules` (§3.6), queued ahead of all reads; `memory.json` becomes `memory.json.migrated`; a failed migration leaves both files and runs empty. The board's `autoReview.rules` are left in place for phase B to drop.

The card summary of such a turn gets the `Guardrails` section and the `correction` field (`shared/summary.ts`, `server/summaries.ts`); `GuardrailsService.learn` keeps card items, promotes the second card into a guideline, counts misses with scope widening, revives paused/archived guidelines, ends a dispute as caught; secrets are recorded as an error, not kept. Events `guardrail_learned` / `guardrail_retired` carry `guardrail: {change, kind, id}` into the flow log.

RPCs `guardrails.read|counts|create|update|delete|detect|retro`; finishing a card closes its items and turns open disputes into misfires.

- Review rework (orchestrator review of bffa7e5, spec 7964f06): card items stay open after Done/Cancelled; the rework quote has no 15-character minimum; `missed` only when the card's latest `review.items` passed or n/a'd the guideline; a match on a merged or superseded guideline resolves along `supersededBy` (`liveEntry`).

- Applies everywhere `SessionMarkdown` renders (card session preview, card descriptions, artifact text). Live rendering stays out of scope by design: plugin timeline transformers only run in the host session view (confirmed again on screen — the same block renders live there).
- One new string in all nine catalogs; fence-detection tests in `client/session-markdown.test.ts`.

Real desktop screenshot of the card session preview showing the collapsed notice: [verification/ui-fence-notice-2026-11-05](verification/ui-fence-notice-2026-11-05/).

## Telemetry error fixes (card "遥测错误分析") — 2026-10-27

- First two days of `$exception` reports (34 events, 35 installs) grouped and mapped to source with `scripts/map-bundle-lines.mjs`: [verification/telemetry-errors-2026-10-27.md](verification/telemetry-errors-2026-10-27.md).
- Local daemon connection (`server/local-daemon.ts`, 13 events): `paseo.pid` addresses are resolved like the `paseo` CLI, including Unix sockets and Windows named pipes (through the bundled `ws`, now a pinned dependency; typed by `server/ws.d.ts` because `@types/ws` breaks the daemon's timer types). An empty address (Paseo restarting the daemon) is `LocalDaemonUnavailable`. Fixes read-state sync, workspace restore, background-workspace reuse, attention clearing, summarizer delete and `paseo_version` on those installs. [WORKSPACE-RESTORE-SPEC](WORKSPACE-RESTORE-SPEC.md)
- Windows CLI (`server/paseo-cli.ts`, 3 events): `paseo.cmd` is started as the command it wraps (`Paseo.exe` + runner + CLI entry, or Node + npm's `bin/paseo`), never through cmd.exe; session settings and native project create/delete share it. [UI-REFRESH-SPEC](UI-REFRESH-SPEC.md)
- Not reported any more (logged as before): `DaemonConnectionError`, `LocalDaemonUnavailable`, `SummaryModelError`. A missing folder or session image is answered with a plain message instead of `ENOENT`. [TELEMETRY-SPEC §11.2](TELEMETRY-SPEC.md)
- Not verified on Linux or Windows hardware; see the report.
- Onboarding: no tour, tip or What's new change; these are fixes to existing behavior.
